  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The checker is right, so the finding stands.
    
    Source: line 86 of results/raw/2026-10-10_m-design-to-code_pre-LOCK-v0.1.md (C9, (ii)) reads `m:330's "at the end"
    includes an end by STOP, so the bars' cumulative counts are evaluated and recorded at the STOP point as the
    campaign's last look, and the verdict sentence names that look.` with ASCII double quotes around "at the end".
    
    The entry: lines 15-16 of entry_d2.md give `C9(ii): "m:330's 'at the end' includes an end by STOP, ... names that
    look".`
    
    Test: I joined the two soft-wrapped lines and ran a Python substring check. The entry's quote is not a substring of
    the rulings (False). With 'at the end' changed back to "at the end" it is a substring (True). There are no non-ASCII
    characters in the quote. So the only difference is the nested-quote swap from double to single marks. Everything
    else is exact, including the attribution to the seat (rulings C9(ii)). The task binds quotes to be character-exact,
    so this swap, though it follows the usual convention, fails that rule. Code's build spec
    (results/raw/2026-10-10_m-d2-c9-build_review.txt l.16) made the same swap, so it is not an independent source for
    the single quotes. RESULTS.md has no earlier entry where nesting forced this choice. The checker's block-quote
    replacement is an exact substring of the rulings (verified True).
  CORRECTION:
    Make the quote character-exact by setting it as a block quote under the list item, keeping the source's inner double
    quotes. The text below was checked as an exact substring of the rulings at l.86:
    
        - C9(ii) (rulings, C9):
    
          > m:330's "at the end" includes an end by STOP, so the bars' cumulative counts are evaluated and recorded at
    the STOP point as the campaign's last look, and the verdict sentence names that look.
    
    The other option is to keep the inline form and add a disclosure, e.g. `(inner quotation marks rendered single)`,
    but the inline text would then still not be character-exact.
--- [H0-29] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    A stopped campaign is never resumed, so a pause raised at a STOP is adjudicated only through the analyzer's
    --adjudication file (the seat's C9(i), C3)
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The behaviour the claim describes is correct. Both ways of continuing a stopped checkpoint are refused:
    valence_core/m/stages.py `_refuse_stopped` ("STAGE ENDED at {where} (STOP): a resume never lifts a STOP") and
    resume.py `refuse_ended`. The `_stop_end_look` PIN says the pause "enters VERDICTS as the declared --adjudication
    file (the seat's C9(i), C3)". The re-finish refusal says the same, and selftest (b) is refused with STAGE ENDED.
    
    The attribution is not exact.
    - The seat's C3 (pre-LOCK rulings, line 69) is general: "VERDICTS refuses while the last pause is unadjudicated; the
    adjudication enters as a declared `--adjudication` file bound to the checkpoint." A pause raised at a STOP is the
    campaign's last pause, so C3 covers it directly.
    - The seat's C9(i) (line 86) is narrower: "The adjudication of a fired end look on a terminal campaign enters as the
    declared `--adjudication` file (C3); `stages.py` need not record it." It answers the first bullet of Code's C9. That
    bullet ("no path for recording a seat adjudication of a fired end look on a terminal campaign") was listed
    separately from the STOP gap ("No end look is evaluated on a STOP-ended campaign"), which became C9(ii).
    - Code's own record treats C9(i) as the terminal case and not the STOP case:
      - stages.TERMINAL = ("COMPLETE","CAP","PI-STOP");
      - resume.refuse_ended's docstring lists "stopped" and "terminal campaign" separately;
      - SEAT_ROUTED says "C9(i) for the end look on a terminal campaign";
      - the build-spec and review raws call C9(i) "that exact terminal-PAUSE case" (review raw, lines 75 and 556–560);
      - the entry itself says "as at a terminal end", which sets terminal apart from STOP;
      - the analyzer's PIN-9 rewrites C9(i) as "terminal (or stopped)", so the "(or stopped)" is Code's addition.
    
    There is a counter-reading, and it does not rescue the citation. m:284 makes a G-VER STOP "a PI stop", m:247 makes a
    PI stop terminal, and the seat's C5 calls the G-VER STOP a "terminal event". But C9(i) answers Code's two-bullet
    split, in which "terminal" excludes STOP. So listing C9(i) as a source for the STOP case is an extension, while C3
    supports it on its own wording.
    
    A smaller point: the flat "A stopped campaign is never resumed" could be read as settling the seat's C2 open
    question ("Whether an adjudicated fire may instead be resumed under the resume identity is a question for the
    adjudication, if one arises; nothing is built for it now"). It holds as a description of stages. The replacement
    wording scopes it to stages.
  CORRECTION:
    stages never resumes a stopped campaign (`resume.refuse_ended`), so a pause raised at a STOP is adjudicated only
    through the analyzer's `--adjudication` file (the seat's C3, "the last pause"; its C9(i) names the same path for a
    fired end look on a terminal campaign).
--- [H0-31] FAILS
  CLAIM:
    A test moving the trigger from round 7 to round 9 gives identical stdout, report and end look
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The checker is right. At b8aa706, valence_core/m/stages.py mentions e_9 at only four lines (3891 is camp9's def;
    4023, 4062-4065).
    
    - Line 4023 runs the case: `e_9 = camp9(root9("c9_e_9"), rule_e(9))`.
    - Lines 4062-4065 are the only assertions on it:
      - e_9 has no exception and its status is STOP.
      - `_resume.same_json(e_9["ck"]["campaign"]["end_look"], el_e)`, where el_e is e_ref's checkpoint end look.
      - `stdout9(e_9["txt"], "c9_e_9") == stdout9(e_ref["txt"], "c9_e_ref")`.
      - A check that the stopped session's round counts differ.
    - No assertion compares e_9["rep"] with e_ref["rep"]. The report checks in ok_e, such as
    `e_ref["rep"].get("end_look") == el_e` and the class scan over json.dumps(e_ref["rep"]), cover e_ref only.
    
    stdout9 is defined as "the run's whole stdout, its out root's name normalised and the stamped PROVENANCE line
    dropped". It replaces root_name with "<root>" and drops lines starting with "PROVENANCE". So the stdout is the same
    only after that normalisation, not identical outright.
    
    _script_policy (stages.py:2575-2584) maps n to the round index, so rule_e(7) and rule_e(9) do move the trigger from
    round 7 to round 9, as the claim says.
    
    The check's own text and the review raw agree:
    - The check's text says the end look is "roster-free and identical, and the whole stdout byte-identical (paths
    normalised, PROVENANCE dropped), when the trigger fires at round 9". It says nothing about the report.
    - Fix-report item 11 (review raw l.1375) reads "whole-stdout identity at round 7 vs round 9".
    
    Report identity across rounds does appear in the review raw (l.827-830), but only from the reviewer's scratch probe
    mut/probe_blind.py (n=5, 7, 9, 10; report "provenance and timing removed"). That is not the selftest, so "A test ...
    gives identical ... report" overclaims.
  CORRECTION:
    A test moving the trigger from round 7 to round 9 gives the same end look and the same stdout (out-root names
    normalised, the PROVENANCE line dropped).

    f7e8aaa6…). Hashing scratchpad/step9/agents/verifier/ganl1 and ganl2/G-ANL-F0.json gives 6f078455… for both.
    - **Closing round's verifier:** §7 (5) "`ganl --out` (scratch dir), run twice" (lines 1667-1672) reports byte-
    identical manifests with sha256 fd55c71351a5a98bb10e5aa42d3b21e8e8efde1fb1afa4c503610a637ecbbbf7. Hashing
    agents2/verifier/ganl1 and ganl2/G-ANL-F0.json gives fd55c713… for both.

--- [H1-47] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    Flag: the posting command computes C7's (a)/(b)/(c) enumeration and lists any residue, renders no PASS/FAIL against
    C7's bar, exits 0 and prints "N enumerated rows" whatever the residue
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The behaviour the claim describes is right. In scripts/m_analyze.py, run_posting (2368-2424) builds its output with
    v1_outputs(...), which calls enumeration(). Its only stdout line is f"POSTING v1: tuple {t}; ceiling ...;
    {len(enumeration)} enumerated rows ({status}: {path.name})". The enumeration rows (row_id, loc, clauses) are written
    to POSTING-v1_outputs.{kind}.json. No PASS/FAIL is computed. main() (6184-6194) prints the lines and returns 0
    unless a refusal is raised, so the exit code does not depend on the residue. The build review raw agrees: line 291
    says "computes the same (a)/(b)/(c) enumeration and lists each residue by row id and location", line 294 says "It
    exits 0 and prints \"N enumerated rows\" whatever the residue", and line 1450 confirms this and calls it "a finding
    for Code only".
    
    The imprecise part is attribution. (a), (b) and (c) are not C7's. They are the clauses of the §7.5 posting rule at
    m:349 ("*(a) No sentence ... *(b) ... *(c) The f1 CORR dose note is carried iff ...*"). In the analyzer,
    enumeration()'s docstring reads "The §7.5 enumeration under the tuple (m:349)". C7, the seat's ruling, only sets a
    bar over that enumeration: "PASS iff, on the posted text, the analyzer's enumeration under (a), (b) and (c) is
    empty". So "C7's (a)/(b)/(c) enumeration" credits the seat's ruling with the enumeration that m:349 defines and
    Code's analyzer computes.
    
    The checker's correction fixes the attribution but drops the claim's second sentence (exit 0, the "N enumerated
    rows" print). That sentence is accurate and should stay.
  CORRECTION:
    **Flagged for Code's own record, not changed (due before the v1 posting):** the `posting` command computes the
    analyzer's §7.5 enumeration under (a), (b) and (c) (m:349), which is the enumeration C7's bar names, and lists any
    residue by row id and location. It renders no PASS/FAIL against C7's bar. It exits 0 and prints "N enumerated rows"
    whatever the residue.

    two sources show it. First, Code's block (results/raw/2026-10-10_m-code-to-design_pre-LOCK-v0.1.md:378) heads the
    item "E5. G-PORT's C1–C9, 'Covered by §2.7, with a consequence the seat may want stated under the item.'" and lists
    C2 (under (6)) as m's audit ending f1's s48 and s25. Second, the G-PORT entry in RESULTS.md (around line 1264) has
    "C2, under (6). **m's audit ends two f1 sessions at the forced phase where f1 continued:** s48 ... and s25". The
    seat's own E3(4) cites "A1, C1 and C2, and C1 is the dose note's own fact (40 of 306 Q rounds byte-clean under f1's
    corrupted label)", which is G-PORT's C1. The draft entry (entry_d2.md:165) says only "E5: as E3(4) states; C2 is
    stated under (6) in Appendix D." It drops the subject ("C1–C9's consequences", i.e. G-PORT's) and the word
    "Accepted". Within the entry, "C2" has already been used for the seat's own C2: line 60 has "PIN-8 (C2)" and line
    147 has "C1–C5 confirmed PIN-5, -8, ...". A reader would therefore take E5's C2 for the seat's PIN-8 ruling, not for
    G-PORT's s48/s25 audit consequence. Nothing in the line is false, but the referent is ambiguous and points the wrong
    way, so the verdict is IMPRECISE rather than FAILS. The correction's parenthetical matches the rulings character for
    character. The "G-PORT's" qualifier is supported by Code's block E5 and by the G-PORT entry.
  CORRECTION:
    E5: G-PORT's C1–C9 consequences accepted as E3(4) states; G-PORT's C2 (m's audit ends f1's s48 and s25 at the forced
    phase, where f1 continued) is stated under (6) in Appendix D.
--- [H1-56] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    Left open: two minor candidates found in verification, not ordered by the seat: VERDICTS does not check that a fired
    end look is matched by a pause, nor that end_look.at_launches equals the launches recorded at the STOP; a hand-
    edited checkpoint would pass both; checkpoints written by stages carry both
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The claim's content checks out, but "found in verification" gives the wrong origin. The checker's correction also
    misplaces one origin.
    
    Content, verified:
    - The closing verifier's residual 5 (review raw, lines 1699-1700) reads "Carried candidate (minor): VERDICTS checks
    neither that a fired end look has a matching pause nor that `end_look.at_launches` equals the launches recorded at
    the STOP. A hand-edited checkpoint would pass."
    - In b8aa706's m_analyze.py, run_verdicts (around lines 1993-2018) has only three relevant checks: the V2 refusal
    when a STOP with sessions has no end_look, PIN-9 on pauses[-1] being unadjudicated, and PIN-8. Nothing checks that a
    pause matches a fired end look, and nothing checks at_launches against len(sessions).
    - In b8aa706's stages.py, `_stop_end_look` returns either the end-is-look result, which carries at_launches equal to
    n, or campaign_look(...,"end"), whose at_launches is len(records). In both branches a fired look comes with a pause.
    The terminal branch does the same. So "checkpoints written by stages carry both" holds.
    - "Not ordered by the seat" holds. The fix agent wrote at raw:1264-1265: "It would be a new refusal the spec and
    rulings do not order; I leave it to Code as a candidate." The first verifier wrote at raw:1486: "the spec does not
    order a fix".
    
    Origin, which is where the claim goes wrong:
    - The pause-match check was first proposed in the REVIEW. The campaign-state lens's PROPOSED FIX (raw:644-645) says
    "Optionally add a V2 structural assertion: at a STOP, a fired end look must be matched by the last pause". The FIX
    REPORT then declined it and left it to Code as a candidate (raw:1264-1265). The first verifier listed it as
    "Candidate (minor; also flagged by the fixer)" (raw:1484-1486), and that verifier covered the pause-match check
    only.
    - The at_launches gap was first raised by a refuter in the review's blinding-and-tests lens (raw:914-915): "does not
    check `end_look.at_launches == len(sessions)`". The closing verifier only carried it, combined with the pause-match
    check.
    - So neither candidate was first found in verification.
    
    The checker's correction needs one fix. It says "raised in the review (the fix agent; a refuter)", but the fix agent
    is not part of the review: the raw has §3 REVIEW and §4 FIX REPORT as separate sections. The pause-match check was
    first raised by the campaign-state reviewer, and the fix agent then left it to Code as a candidate.
  CORRECTION:
    Two minor candidates, not ordered by the seat: VERDICTS does not check that a fired end look is matched by a pause,
    nor that `end_look.at_launches` equals the launches recorded at the STOP. Both came up in the review. The first was
    an optional fix proposed by the campaign-state lens, which the fix agent left to Code as a candidate. The second was
    raised by a refuter in the blinding-and-tests lens. The verifiers carried them forward: the first verifier listed
    the first, and the closing verifier listed both. A hand-edited checkpoint would pass both checks; checkpoints
    written by stages satisfy both.

    -- flagged for the seat: whether m:271's withholding extends to campaign looks."
    (2) The same PIN appears in results/raw/2026-10-06_m-build_pins.txt:855-857. That file's header says of all 194 PIN
    sites: "Each unit's LOCK v0.1 is to declare them (LOCK-m-F0 v0.1 at §8 step 9".
    (3) m:279 has LOCK-m-F0 v0.1 declare "every bar of every later Q-F0 stage with class, unit, p0, α, domain and
    cadence (campaign: §6)". m:330's round_degeneracy bar is "domain the unit's sham arms, reported on its contrast
    arms". What a look reports on the contrast arm is therefore part of what LOCK v0.1 declares. LOCK v1.0's list at
    m:286 (launch counts, hour cap, G-PWR, D1, hashes, sentence table, decisions block) does not include it.
    (4) The seat's D1 and D2 (design-to-code raw lines 90 and 92) have the lock state the package_sha256 and the D2
    edit's diff, and check the package hash against the lock at stage start. stages.py is in that package, so answering
    the flag in code would move the package that LOCK v0.1 binds.
    The true part: campaign looks first run at step 16, after LOCK-m-F0 v1.0 is committed at step 15 (m:358). The flag
    itself is step-3 note 126 (results/raw/2026-10-06_m-v1.11_step3_notes.md:136), and the seat's rulings do not address
    it.
    So the sentence states as fact a scope point that the sources contradict, and it settles a timing question that
    belongs to the seat.
  CORRECTION:
    It bears on campaign looks, which first run under the unit's LOCK v1.0 (m:358 steps 15-16). The PIN, though, is one
    of the markers each unit's LOCK v0.1 is to declare ("declared at LOCK v0.1"; `2026-10-06_m-build_pins.txt`). LOCK
    v0.1 also declares every later Q-F0 bar's domain and cadence, the campaign's included (m:279), and `stages.py` is in
    the package LOCK v0.1 names (D2). So whether the flag must be answered before LOCK v0.1 is the seat's to say.
--- [H1-60] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    It also carries the G-SHAPE-F0 register statements (B2, B3), on Code's reading above unless the seat directs
    otherwise
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    I checked the claim against the rulings raw and agree it is IMPRECISE, though some of the checker's reasoning does
    not hold.
    
    1. The seat's ruling really can be read two ways. B2 says "State that in the artifact's register." B3 says "a known
    limit, stated in the register". But data/m/gates/G-SHAPE-F0.json has no register key; a search for "register" in it
    finds nothing. PIN-S1 to S10 exist only as "PIN (Code, declared at LOCK v0.1; ...)" comments in
    valence_core/m/gshape.py.
    
    2. Code's reading (the lock's register) has real support. A4 says "G-SHAPE-F0's artifact and G-ANL-F0's manifest
    stand as committed, subject only to D2". B3 says "All confirmed; no re-run on their account". The closing list "What
    Code does now, in order" puts no gshape.py edit or G-SHAPE-F0 re-run before the lock. So the checker's use of A2 is
    weak taken literally: Code's reading does not depart from the listed order.
    
    3. The two readings still change what happens before the lock.
       - Under the other reading, gshape.py is edited. gshape.py is in G-SHAPE-F0's tooling map (provenance.tooling =
    ['scripts/m_analyze... no: 'scripts/m_sessions.py', 'valence_core/m/gshape.py']).
       - PIN-S10 (gshape.py, around line 233) and D3 both say "any difference re-runs G-SHAPE-F0 first".
       - The same edit moves package_sha256. Code's own block, line 194: "A replaced reading edits gshape.py, moves
    `package_sha256` and re-runs G-SHAPE-F0 before the lock." That would also change the 750b24ed hash that the entry's
    headline (line 8) says the lock must name.
    
    4. The seat does not see the lock before it is committed. The rulings say the lock is "Code's to draft and commit on
    the PI's word; the seat reads it at step 10's ping".
    
    5. So "on Code's reading above unless the seat directs otherwise" means Code acts on its own reading of the seat's
    ambiguous word unless someone objects. That settles, by default, something that belongs to the seat. It also sits
    badly with the entry's own flag (line 141, "before the lock is drafted"; line 145, "Code has not acted on either
    reading").
    
    6. The checker's correction is itself not exactly right.
       - It says the lock "carries ... in the register the seat means". Under the other reading the lock does not carry
    the statements; the re-run G-SHAPE-F0 artifact does.
       - It says "before the lock is committed", which does not match the entry's own flag ("before the lock is
    drafted").
       - It leaves out that the other reading also moves package_sha256.
    
    The replacement below avoids these problems and agrees with lines 141–145, with D3 and PIN-S10, and with Code's
    block line 194.
  CORRECTION:
    Whether it also carries the G-SHAPE-F0 register statements (B2, B3) waits on the seat's answer to Code's reading
    flagged above, put to the seat through the PI before the lock is drafted: on that reading LOCK v0.1 states both in
    its G-SHAPE-F0 register; on the other, `gshape.py` is edited, `package_sha256` moves, and G-SHAPE-F0 re-runs first
    (D3).

============================================================ CONFIRMED CORRECTIONS
--- [H0-21] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    The licence 'now also checks' (d) that the analyzer's bytes hash to analyzer_sha256
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The checker is right; I checked the code myself. At 8402174, `valence_core/m/stages.py` `licence_problems` already
    had check (d). Its docstring says "(d) when identity_extra pins ``analyzer_sha256``, the analyzer's bytes hash to it
    (finding R8)". The code runs it under `want_ana = extra.get("analyzer_sha256")` / `if want_ana is not None:`, and
    `analyzer_sha256` was in `_OPTIONAL_EXTRA` at that commit, so the key was optional. At 185deb0 the docstring reads
    "(d) the analyzer's bytes hash to identity_extra's ``analyzer_sha256`` (finding R8); (e) ... (f) ... (d)-(f) are the
    seat's D2 (2026-10-10): both keys are required on a non-stub run, so an absent or malformed one is a problem here".
    A new loop over ("analyzer_sha256", "package_sha256") adds a problem for any key that is absent or not 64 lower-case
    hex. The hash comparisons (d) and (e), and the lock-text check (f), then run only for keys that pass that test
    (`have`); (f) also needs the lock's committed bytes (`lock_data`). The spec in
    results/raw/2026-10-10_m-d2-c9-build_review.txt:31 also calls it "(d) existing: ... now always present on a non-stub
    run". So "It now also checks: (d)" wrongly makes (d) sound new. What is new: (d) is no longer conditional, an absent
    or malformed key is itself a problem, and (e) and (f) are new. One fix to the checker's correction: "runs (d) on
    every non-stub run" is slightly off too. With an absent or malformed `analyzer_sha256`, the hash comparison does not
    run; a key problem is recorded instead.
  CORRECTION:
    Replace "qualification, the campaign, and a re-finish. It now also checks:" and its three bullets with:
    "qualification, the campaign, and a re-finish. Before the edit it checked the analyzer's bytes against
    `analyzer_sha256` only when that key was given. Now both keys are required there, an absent or malformed one is
    itself a problem, and for well-formed keys it checks: - (d) that the analyzer's bytes hash to `analyzer_sha256` (the
    existing check, no longer conditional); - (e) that `resume.package_sha256()` equals `package_sha256` (new); - (f)
    that the committed lock's text names both 64-hex strings verbatim, as the analyzer's PIN-14 already requires of the
    lock (new)."
--- [H0-25] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    The LICENCE line names both hashes, and claims for them only what was checked
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The working tree matches 185deb0. In valence_core/m/stages.py, _licence (lines 624-628) prints f"...; analyzer
    {extra['analyzer_sha256'][:16]} and package {extra['package_sha256'][:16]}: the hashes identity_extra and the lock
    name, hashed from the working tree at stage start". So the line shows the 16-hex prefixes, not the full 64-hex
    hashes. The code's own PIN (lines 616-617) says "the LICENCE line names the package's sha16 after the analyzer's".
    The self-test texts (lines 3504, 4377, 4384) say "(the analyzer's and the package's sha16)". The build-review raw
    (results/raw/2026-10-10_m-d2-c9-build_review.txt, lines 1526-1527) separates the two: the line names "the analyzer's
    and package's sha16", while "The checkpoint's `licence` records both hashes". The entry's own neighbouring lines use
    "names" for full strings: (f) "names both 64-hex strings verbatim" (line 39), and package_sha256 is "recorded in the
    checkpoint" through LICENCE_KEYS (line 41). Read in that context, "names both hashes" suggests the full hashes,
    which is wrong. Before b8aa706 the line printed only an optional analyzer sha16, plus "committed at HEAD, working
    tree identical" over everything. The second half of the claim holds. licence_problems checks (d) the analyzer's
    bytes, read from ANALYZER_PATH on disk, against analyzer_sha256; (e) resume.package_sha256(), the working-tree
    valence_core/m/*.py, against package_sha256; and (f) the committed lock text containing both full strings. The line
    now claims only "the hashes identity_extra and the lock name, hashed from the working tree", and no longer says the
    analyzer or package is committed. This was the build review's minor finding 1, fixed by option (b). The checker's
    correction is accurate.
  CORRECTION:
    The LICENCE line names both hashes by their sha16, and claims for them only what was checked.
--- [H0-32] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    So a pause raised at a STOP is adjudicated only through the analyzer's --adjudication file
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The claim describes the build correctly, but its wording settles a route that is still open, so the checker stands.
    
    What the build does at 185deb0:
    - stages.py: `_begin` calls `_resume.refuse_ended(cpath)`. `_refinish_campaign` refuses NOT APPLIED for an ended
    campaign and names "the declared --adjudication file".
    - m_analyze.py, about line 2003: VERDICTS refuses while the last pause is unadjudicated unless a matching
    `--adjudication` doc is supplied. "REFUSED: the campaign's last pause is unadjudicated; the seat's adjudication
    enters as a declared --adjudication file (PIN-9)".
    - The PI-stop record does not take the place of an adjudication. The battery at about lines 4902-4926 refuses "with
    the PI-stop record only (PIN-9)". So "only" holds in the build. PIN-8's record answers the hair-trigger STOP, not
    the pause.
    
    Why the wording is still imprecise:
    1. "So" makes the route follow from stages' refusal alone. It does not. It follows from the analyzer's PIN-9 design,
    which is Code's reading. The 185deb0 PIN text says so: "its use for a pause raised at a STOP is Code's reading,
    flagged for the seat". The block's item 2 also says the reading can be struck, and the analyzer would then change
    ("If the seat strikes it before the lock: the analyzer changes (PIN-9...").
    2. "is adjudicated only through" makes the file the place of adjudication. The seat's C3 says the adjudication
    "enters as a declared `--adjudication` file". The code's PIN says "its adjudication enters VERDICTS as the declared
    --adjudication file". The adjudication is the seat's act; the file is how it enters.
    
    The next sentence does attribute the route to Code. But the sentence under review states it in the indicative as a
    consequence before that. This is exactly the kind of place where Code settles what is the seat's. The checker's
    replacement is accurate on every point: "as built", "a ruling ... can enter", and "only" all check against the code.
  CORRECTION:
    So, as built, a ruling on a pause raised at a STOP can enter only through the analyzer's `--adjudication` file.
--- [H0-38] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    Where the re-finished end leaves the campaign ended (a STOP, or a terminal end with a fired end look's pause) it
    names --adjudication and --pi-stop instead of 'offer it again'; a look pause that does not end the campaign keeps
    the old wording
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The checker is right. In `_refinish_campaign` at 185deb0 (valence_core/m/stages.py, about lines 2496-2510) the
    switch is `ended = ("STOP" if pending.get("stop") is not None else (pending.get("terminal") or {}).get("status"))`,
    followed by `if skipped and ended is not None:`, which raises the new wording naming "the declared --adjudication
    file" and "the declared --pi-stop record". Otherwise it falls through to the old "offer it again" message.
    
    **Where `pending["terminal"]` is set.** `_campaign_end` (about lines 2220-2257) sets it for every status in TERMINAL
    = ("COMPLETE", "CAP", "PI-STOP") (line 159), whether the end look fired or not. It is also set when the end look is
    the look already recorded at a multiple of 40, which raises no new pause. A pending COMPLETE or CAP end with no
    pause, re-finished with an adjudication offered, gives `ended = "COMPLETE"` (or "CAP") and so gets the new wording.
    A pending PI-STOP re-finished with an adjudication offered gets it too; in that case `pi_stop` is passed as None
    because the checkpoint already records it (line 2095). The PIN at lines 2483-2484 says the same thing: "its stop or
    terminal state persisted: resume.ended_status non-None".
    
    **Where the narrower wording comes from.** The claim's "a terminal end with a fired end look's pause" matches the
    docstring's narrower "(a STOP, or a terminal state beside a fired end look's pause)". It does not match the branch.
    The fix report (build_review.txt:1237-1238) says "`pending.stop` is set or `pending.terminal` is set". Check (j)
    (lines 4224-4272) tests only a STOP whose end look fired, a COMPLETE whose end look fired, and a look pause. The
    case of a terminal end with no pause is untested, but the code clearly sends it to the new wording.
    
    **The second sentence is true but incomplete.** A `schedule_exhausted` pause (line 2144) goes through the `status ==
    "PAUSE"` branch with stop and terminal both None, so it also keeps "offer it again". The fix report confirms this at
    line 1242: "A look pause or exhausted schedule that is not ended keeps the old wording".
    
    **The STOP part is correct.** It covers any STOP, the G-VER (harness) kind included, because `pending.stop` is set
    for all of them.
  CORRECTION:
    The re-finish's NOT APPLIED refusal (from a review nit) changed. Where the re-finished end leaves the campaign ended
    (a STOP, or a terminal state, COMPLETE, CAP or a PI stop, whether or not a fired end look's pause stands beside it),
    it now names the declared `--adjudication` and `--pi-stop` routes instead of "offer it again". A pause that does not
    end the campaign (a look pause or an exhausted schedule) keeps the old wording.

--- [H1-69] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    L223-224: after the lock, each analyzer edit would move the analyzer sha256 the lock hashes, re-run G-ANL-F0 and
    supersede its manifest again
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    entry_d2.md L222-224 (item 4) reads: "Whether to build, before the lock, any of the following analyzer edits. After
    the lock, each would move the analyzer sha256 the lock hashes, re-run G-ANL-F0 and supersede its manifest again." I
    re-checked it against the code and the rulings and found two problems.
    
    (1) It leaves out the main consequence of an edit after the lock. The D2 build's licence_problems
    (valence_core/m/stages.py L528-603) is reached through _licensed/_licence by every stage: run_gate_stage L1465,
    run_gid_pair L1729 and run_campaign L2033. Its check (d) compares the analyzer's bytes with identity_extra's
    analyzer_sha256. Its check (f) requires the committed lock text to contain that same 64-hex string. After an
    analyzer edit, one of the two always fails. If identity_extra keeps the old hash, (d) fails; if it carries the new
    one, (f) fails. So every non-stub stage refuses until a lock revision names the new hash. The entry never says this;
    grep finds no refusal or revision wording near L224. Code's own round-2 block (item 4) does say it: "Made after, the
    lock's analyzer hash also moves, so every non-stub stage refuses (D2) until a lock revision names the new sha."
    
    (2) It places the re-run and the supersession under "After the lock", and has the edit itself perform them. The same
    block says these happen "Either way": before the lock, `c84a052b…16e0` would be superseded. An edit does not re-run
    anything. PIN-37 (m_analyze.py L2618 and L1774-1776) says an artifact is "moved aside by hand with `git mv` to a
    dated superseded name on the seat's ruling". write_texts_once refuses to write a different G-ANL-F0.json until that
    is done. The manifest records analyzer.sha256 0da9115b…bf0f, so any analyzer edit makes it stale. D2 ordered the re-
    run and supersession only for the D2 edit. For other edits it is a precedent, not a rule Code can state as settled.
    
    The first clause, that an edit after the lock would move the analyzer sha256 the lock hashes, is correct: D2 says
    "the lock hashes the re-run's manifest and analyzer".
    
    The checker's wording is mostly right. However, "as D2 ordered for this edit" is ambiguous inside item 4, which is
    about other edits. It also keeps the re-run and supersession in the after-the-lock frame.
  CORRECTION:
    Replace L223-224's sentence with: "Either way, each moves the analyzer sha256, and by D2's precedent G-ANL-F0 would
    be re-run and its manifest superseded again by hand on the seat's ruling (PIN-37). After the lock, it would also
    move the analyzer sha256 the lock hashes, so every non-stub stage would refuse (D2) until a lock revision names the
    new one."

    left it to Code (fix report: "I leave it to Code as a candidate"). The first verifier carried it as Residual 2
    (l.1484): "VERDICTS never checks that a fired end look is matched by a pause". The closing verifier carried it as
    Residual 5 (l.1699).
    
    (2) at_launches candidate. It was raised by the REFUTER of the blinding-and-tests lens's substantive finding
    (l.912-915): "does not check `end_look.at_launches == len(sessions)`". Searches for "at_launches", "candidate",
    "matched" and "hand-edit" turn up nothing about it in the first verifier's report (l.1347-1500); that report's only
    at_launches hit (l.1389) is about test coverage. The fix report and the closing fix report do not carry it either.
    Only the closing verifier carries it, in Residual 5 (l.1699-1700): "VERDICTS checks neither that a fired end look
    has a matching pause nor that `end_look.at_launches` equals the launches recorded at the STOP. A hand-edited
    checkpoint would pass."
    
    So "carried by both verifiers" is wrong for (2). The round-2 block (block_round2.md l.122-125) already has the split
    right: "(b) ... both verifiers carried it; (c) was raised by a refuter in the blinding-and-tests lens, and the
    closing round's verifier carried it." The entry contradicts its own block. The checker's proposed correction is
    accurate. Below it is reworded so each origin sits beside the candidate it belongs to, and the trailing sentence is
    kept.
  CORRECTION:
    - Two minor candidates from the review: VERDICTS does not check that a fired end look is matched by a pause (the
    campaign-state lens's optional fix; both verifiers carried it), nor that `end_look.at_launches` equals the launches
    recorded at the STOP (raised by a refuter in the blinding-and-tests lens; the closing round's verifier carried it).
    A hand-edited checkpoint would pass both; checkpoints written by stages satisfy both.

    seat's orders to the lock. The "Then" bullet keeps "the lock hashes the re-run's manifest and analyzer". So leaving
    out D3's order to the lock is an omission in a list meant to cover the seat's orders.
    - **Mitigating, but not enough:** the entry does mention this order elsewhere ("stating the `stages.py` diff (D1,
    D3)", l.249-251). That makes the omission local, not across the whole entry, but the D3 paraphrase on its own is
    still incomplete.

  CLAIM:
    These text edits were not ordered. The seat's D2 licensed an analyzer edit in this round only where C9(ii) touches
    the sentence.
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    I checked results/raw/2026-10-10_m-design-to-code_pre-LOCK-v0.1.md in full.
    
    - **"Not ordered" holds.** The short form says "One edit is ordered before LOCK v0.1 — Code's own D2 proposal, with
    the end-look gap of C9 folded in". The rulings around the analyzer do not order PIN-text changes:

    required in the stages' `identity_extra` on non-stub runs ... The edit to `stages.py` also closes C9(ii) (the end
    look at a STOP); if C9(ii) touches the analyzer's sentence, that edit lands in the same round. G-ANL-F0 is then

    - **Code's own D2 proposal touches only stages.py.** In 2026-10-10_m-code-to-design_pre-LOCK-v0.1.md (line 338 on)
    it says "This is a `stages.py` change". So the only analyzer edit the seat names in this round is the one that
    depends on C9(ii).

    edits are excluded. "Licensed an analyzer edit ... only where" therefore reads a limit into the seat's words that
    the text does not contain. The claim's substance is right but the wording overreaches, so the checker's IMPRECISE
    stands.
    - **The checker's correction is acceptable but loose.** Its quote is exact. However, "names an analyzer edit in this
    round only where" can still be read as the seat restricting edits. A tighter wording states what D2 names, the
    condition, and the quote.

    to pre-D2 packages". Its FAILURE SCENARIO (l.458) opens "LOCK v0.2 (qualification) or LOCK v1.0 (campaign) restates
    LOCK v0.1's package 8692f47c...".
    
    (2) The refuter never uses the word "harmless". It attributes the LOCK v0.1 point to the finding: "As the finding
    also concedes, for LOCK v0.1 every extra hash belongs to a pre-D2 package" (l.491). It mentions the v0.2/v1.0
    scenario only to call it "hypothetical and outside this build's scope" (l.494-495).
    
    (3) The note is the refuter's: "What survives is a reasonable note for whoever drafts the later locks (Code): either
    give the hashes of record a single declared line form, or write historical hashes truncated" (l.503-504). The note
    itself contains no scenario.
    
    In the draft (entry_d2.md l.123-125), "Its" and "it" follow straight after "The refuter's note", so they read as the
    note's or the refuter's. That gives both the scenario and the "harmless" wording to the wrong party.
    
    In the checker's correction, "the refuter agrees" is ambiguous after a two-part sentence. The refuter agrees only on
    LOCK v0.1. It calls the v0.2/v1.0 scenario hypothetical and out of scope, so the replacement says "agrees on LOCK
    v0.1". The next draft sentence ("The note: declare one line form...") still follows correctly after the replacement.
  CORRECTION:
    1 was refuted: that check (f) passes any full 64-hex hash the lock text contains. The finding's own scenario is LOCK
    v0.2 or v1.0. The finding itself calls the substring test harmless for LOCK v0.1, whose extra hashes all belong to

  CLAIM:
    L98-99: Code's three text-only follow-ups came after this entry's values-check passes found texts crediting the seat
    with more than its words.
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    I re-checked this myself and the checker is right. The lead line (entry_d2.md L98-99) says all three follow-ups came
    after this entry's values-check passes "found texts crediting the seat with more than its words". That holds for the
    first two and not for the third.

    this up:

    STOP.
      - Another finding covers PIN-6 and PIN-12 saying "confirmed by the seat (C7, 2026-10-10)" where C7 had "settled"
    the point.
    - **97d70bd (15:59).** Its message says "found by the D2 round entry's second values-check pass". vc_d2_p2.json has
    the matching findings:

      - the NOT APPLIED message still crediting "the seat's C3" for a STOP;
      - the battery comment still saying "confirmed by the seat (C7, ...)".
    - **b7737ef (16:26), after pass 3 (vc_d2_p3.json, 16:16).** Its message says it fixes "every remaining text an
    exhaustive read-only sweep found crediting the seat with more than its words, and one stale PIN". It cites no pass.
    Pass 3's 7 confirmed findings and 13 values findings are all about the entry's own wording, and none names a code
    text that b7737ef changed:
      - nothing on m:330's pause at a STOP;
      - nothing on the analyzer half of the stage-start check (R8, PIN-14 precedent);
      - nothing on the _campaign_events or _stop_end_look PINs;
      - nothing on campaign_look's stale "flagged for the seat".
    
    The entry's own sub-bullet (L120-121) also says b7737ef came "after an exhaustive read-only sweep ... made to end
    the pass-by-pass discoveries". So the lead line credits the passes with finding the third follow-up's texts, when
    Code's sweep found them. The passes' earlier findings only prompted that sweep.
    
    The checker's correction is accurate on every point I checked: pass numbering, the two commit messages, and "for the
    rest", which matches "every remaining text". I tighten its wording slightly below so it states what the sweep looked
    for.

    texts crediting the seat with more than its words, and the third after Code's own exhaustive sweep for any such

    "`analyzer_sha256` and `package_sha256` are required in the stages' `identity_extra` on non-stub runs, and the
    package hash is checked against the lock at stage start." It orders three things: both keys present, and the package
    hash checked against the lock. It orders no check of the analyzer against the lock. D2 also says "the lock hashes
    the re-run's manifest and analyzer", but that is about what the lock contains. It is not a stage refusal.

    valence_core/m/*.py (resume.py:296-328), so the package hash does not move and D2's checks (e) and (f)-package still
    pass. A stage started after the edit is refused by one of two checks in `licence_problems` (stages.py:530-608):
       - (d), if identity_extra still carries the old analyzer hash: the analyzer's bytes no longer match it.
       - (f)'s analyzer half, if identity_extra carries the new hash: the committed lock does not name it. This is the
    one that holds "until a lock revision names the new analyzer."

    stub run and the package hash checked against the lock at stage start ((e), and (f) for the package); (d) (finding
    R8) and (f)'s analyzer half (the PIN-14 precedent) are Code's." The refusal message reads "(the package: the seat's
    D2; the analyzer: Code's, after the analyzer's PIN-14)". The `_licence` docstring reads "the analyzer's half Code's,
    finding R8 / PIN-14". The commit message reads: "The analyzer's half of the stage-start check is Code's (finding R8;
    the analyzer's PIN-14 precedent)."
    
    4. The entry contradicts itself. It credits (f)'s analyzer half to Code at l.42-46, and at l.125-126 it describes
    b7737ef as marking "The analyzer half of the stage-start check, as Code's (finding R8; PIN-14's precedent) and not
    D2's". Then l.322-323 credits the same refusal to D2.
    
    Related issue, outside this claim: l.305-306 ("Every non-stub stage then refuses (D2) until a lock revision names
    the new one") covers items 1-3 together. D2 is right for an item-2 answer that changes stages.py, since that moves
    the package. It is not right for an item-3 analyzer answer, where the refusal is Code's.

    Built after, it does the same, and every non-stub stage refuses until a lock revision names the new analyzer (the
    analyzer half of the stage-start check: Code's, finding R8 and PIN-14's precedent; not D2's).

--- [H0-16] FAILS
  CLAIM:
    Line 17: 'Each says what Code would do without an answer'.
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    I re-read block_round2.md myself. Line 17 says "Each says what Code would do without an answer, and none of them has
    been acted on." Items 1, 3 and 4 each have that sentence:
    - line 35: "Without an answer, Code drafts nothing that depends on it."
    - line 65: "Without an answer, Code declares the PIN at LOCK v0.1 as it stands, still flagged."
    - lines 84-85: "Without an answer, Code builds none of them before the lock."
    
    Item 2 (lines 37-49) has no such sentence. It ends at line 48: "The seat may strike the reading. Striking it would
    change the analyzer, re-run G-ANL-F0 and supersede its manifest before the lock." Nothing in it says what Code does
    if the seat is silent. The claim is false, and the checker was right.
    
    I checked the code to find item 2's real default:
    - At 185deb0, the PIN-9 comment in scripts/m_analyze.py (line 2005) reads "PIN (Code, declared at LOCK v0.1): PIN-9"
    and ends "Code's reading, flagged for the seat: a pause raised at a STOP (the end look of the seat's C9(ii)) is the
    campaign's last pause, so C3's route covers it too."
    - The PINS["PIN-9"] register string says the same.
    - The reading is also in two stages.py PIN comments, each "declared at LOCK v0.1": `_stop_end_look` at about line
    2285 ("its use for a pause raised at a STOP is Code's reading, flagged for the seat") and `_refinish_campaign` at
    about line 2482 ("a STOP's pause by Code's reading of C3, flagged for the seat").
    
    So the as-built state is the default, the same pattern as item 3. The checker's first fix, which only names PIN-9,
    is true but leaves out the stages.py PINs that carry the same reading.
    
    The checker's second fix (rewording line 17 to "item 2's reading is already built and stands…") clashes with the
    rest of line 17, ", and none of them has been acted on." Dropped in as written, it would read as a contradiction. So
    that option is not exactly right.
  CORRECTION:
    Keep line 17 as it is. Add a default sentence to item 2, after line 49, in the same form as item 3's line 65:
    "Without an answer, Code declares PIN-9 and the `stages.py` PINs that carry the reading at LOCK v0.1 as they stand,
    the reading still flagged; the analyzer is not edited." This matches the code at 185deb0: PIN-9 in m_analyze.py and
    the `_stop_end_look` and `_refinish_campaign` PINs in stages.py are each marked "declared at LOCK v0.1" and each
    flags the STOP routing as Code's reading. Do not use the checker's line-17 rewording on its own, because it
    contradicts the "none of them has been acted on" clause that follows it.

    4. "Later" suggests that routing now costs nothing. A change made now also moves the package and re-runs G-ANL-F0,

--- [H0-11]
    Lines 11–12: 'The earlier manifest was moved by `git mv` to `G-ANL-F0.superseded-2026-10-10.json`.'
  REFUTER:
    I re-checked the claim myself and it is correct as written.
    
    (1) The move happened as described. The re-run raw `results/raw/2026-10-10_m-ganl-f0-rerun.txt` line 3 records "git
    mv data/m/gates/G-ANL-F0.json -> data/m/gates/G-ANL-F0.superseded-2026-10-10.json, staged; sha256 9bea8339…7904".
    Line 404 records "(git mv, then unstaged; committed with the entry)".
    
    (2) The file is the right one. `shasum -a 256` of the superseded file is
    9bea83399ac698ef1ede2556d68d0ee4345f8f14468b4fb86c8afb63ec787904, the same as `git show
    HEAD:data/m/gates/G-ANL-F0.json`. So "the earlier manifest" is the manifest of record. The set-aside first re-run
    (fd55c713…bbf7) went to the scratchpad, not to this path (raw line 403), and the block never mentions it, so the
    phrase is not ambiguous.
    
    (3) The checker is right about the index: `git diff --cached` is empty, the superseded file is `??`, and
    G-ANL-F0.json is ` M`. But that does not make the sentence wrong. The sentence describes the action ("was moved by
    `git mv`"), not the current index. The unstaging is a temporary state during checking. The entry (line 240) says the
    superseded file is committed together with the block.
    
    (4) Git stores snapshots, not renames. The re-run wrote a new G-ANL-F0.json at the original path, so the commit will
    be the same whether or not the `git mv` had stayed staged: G-ANL-F0.json modified and
    G-ANL-F0.superseded-2026-10-10.json added (shown as C100 under `git show -C`; plain -M finds no rename in either
    case, because the original path is not deleted). The "staged or committed rename" the checker worries a reader would
    assume is no different from what will be committed. So the omission changes nothing about the record. The block's
    "Where things stand" section describes the state at commit, and at commit the move is committed.
    
    The checker's proposed wording adds true detail, but the original is not imprecise. The block also points to the
    entry, which carries that detail ("Everything is in the D2 round's RESULTS entry").

--- [H2-21] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    (l.96-97) As built, a campaign look reports, per contrast arm, pooled pre-pick exclusion counts, the first-read
    flags of domain sessions, and forced-round degeneracy.
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The checker's verdict holds. At HEAD 97d70bd, `campaign_look` in valence_core/m/stages.py runs from l.1948 (def) to
    l.1978 (return).
    
    What it computes for the unit's one contrast arm (`c_arm, s_arm = keys.templates(unit)`; keys.py:71-76 returns a
    2-tuple):
    - `arms[c_arm]` = {"launched": len(con), "prepick_excluded": ..., "domain": ..., "read_flags": _read_flags(con,
    first_only=True)}. The read flags carry scope "first reads of domain sessions", reads, low_label_mass and
    top1_not_label (l.1929-1945, l.1972-1975).
    - `results` holds `fis.evaluate(kc, len(con), template=c_arm, look=look, sham_k=ks, sham_n=len(sh))` and
    `ab.evaluate(kc, len(con), ...)`. These are the Fisher and absolute guards, both evaluated on the contrast arm's
    pre-pick count kc (l.1960-1963).
    - `contrast_forced_degeneracy` = {"k": kf, "n": len(forced)}, computed over forced rounds only (l.1969-1971, 1978).
    - The PIN at l.1966-1968 keeps free-round events withheld.
    
    Every item the claim lists is right: the pooled pre-pick exclusion count, the first-read flags of domain sessions,
    forced-round degeneracy, and the withholding. But the claim reads as a complete list. It leaves out three things the
    look reports for that arm: its launch count, its domain count, and the two guard results computed on its pre-pick
    count. The self-test at l.3014-3016 asserts launched/prepick_excluded/domain = 20/8/12, and l.3020-3023 checks the
    guard outcomes. Item 3 asks the seat about a pick channel at looks, so an incomplete list of what is reported is a
    substantive gap, not a stylistic one.
    
    Two things are fine:
    - "per contrast arm" matches m:316 and the docstring (one contrast arm per unit).
    - The domain count is not a leak. The stub (l.2999) sets `"domain": not prepick`, and m:308 defines the domain
    before the first read, so the domain count carries no free-round information. Including it does not undercut the
    withholding sentence.
    
    The checker's correction is accurate. Its one weakness is that it presents the guards only as being "evaluated on"
    the count, not as reported results, and it does not say the Fisher guard compares against the sham arm's count. My
    wording below fixes both.
  CORRECTION:
    - **As built**, a campaign look reports for the contrast arm: its launches, its domain count, its pooled pre-pick
    exclusion count, the Fisher guard (against the sham arm's count) and the absolute guard evaluated on that count, the
    read flags of first reads of domain sessions, and forced-round degeneracy (k, n). The arm's free-round events stay
    withheld.

  CLAIM:
    (l.112-113) Made after, the lock's analyzer hash also moves, so every non-stub stage refuses (D2) until a lock
    revision names the new sha.
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The block's l.111-113 read: "Made before LOCK v0.1, that happens to `8bade692…a576` now. Made after, the lock's
    analyzer hash also moves, so every non-stub stage refuses (D2) until a lock revision names the new sha."
    
    1. The lock holds a fixed analyzer hash. Per D2 in results/raw/2026-10-10_m-design-to-code_pre-LOCK-v0.1.md:92, "the
    lock hashes the re-run's manifest and analyzer". That hash is text inside the committed lock file. An edit to
    m_analyze.py changes the analyzer's bytes and leaves the lock's text alone. So "the lock's analyzer hash also moves"
    is literally false: the analyzer moves off the hash the lock names. Read the other way ("the hash of the lock's
    analyzer"), the sentence is ambiguous, and "also" adds nothing, because the sentence before has already said the
    analyzer sha256 moves.
    
    2. The consequence is right. In licence_problems (valence_core/m/stages.py:528-603 at HEAD 97d70bd), check (d) at
    l.582-590 compares the analyzer's bytes with identity_extra's analyzer_sha256, and check (f) at l.598-602 requires
    the committed lock text to contain that sha. If identity_extra keeps the old sha, (d) fails. If it names the new
    sha, (f) fails until a lock revision names it.
    
    3. "Every non-stub stage" holds. _licence (l.606) raises "LICENCE REFUSED" and is called through _licensed
    (l.1127-1132), and all three stage entry points call _licensed: run_gate_stage (l.1465), run_gid_pair (l.1729) and
    run_campaign (l.2033). _refinish_campaign is reached only from run_campaign (l.2093), after its _licensed call.
    
    4. Side checks: sha256 of data/m/gates/G-ANL-F0.json is 8bade692…a576 (it matches the rerun raw at l.1193). The
    analyzer at HEAD and in the working tree hashes to 8e709582…473b.
    
    The checker's correction is accurate.
  CORRECTION:
    Made after, the analyzer no longer hashes to the sha the lock names, so every non-stub stage refuses (D2) until a
    lock revision names the new sha.

============================================================ CONFIRMED CORRECTIONS
--- [H0-18] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    L27-28: the rulings set no precondition for LOCK v0.1 beyond the PI's word ("LOCK v0.1 is then Code's to draft and
    commit on the PI's word")
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The checker is right that the claim is imprecise, and I could not refute that. The quote is exact: the rulings at L5
    read "LOCK v0.1 is then Code's to draft and commit on the PI's word". But the rulings do set preconditions before
    the lock:
    - L5: "One edit is ordered before LOCK v0.1".
    - L92 (D2): "One round, before LOCK v0.1."
    - L94 (D3): "any other difference re-runs G-SHAPE-F0 first."
    - L114-118 ("What Code does now, in order"): step 1 (G-CAL's close entry) and step 2 (the D2 edit with C9(ii), the
    G-ANL-F0 re-run, the git mv, D3's comparison) both come before step 3 (the lock "on the PI's word").
    
    So "no precondition beyond the PI's word" is false as written, even though those steps are now done. Things that
    really are not conditions are named as such elsewhere in the rulings (A9's key-blind read: "none is a condition of
    the lock"; C6). That makes the absolute wording worse, not better.
    
    The checker's own correction is still imprecise in two ways:
    1. It names only "the D2 round". It leaves out step 1, G-CAL's close entry, which is also ordered first (done at
    8402174, block L9).
    2. It calls everything "done". D3's condition is not discharged once and for all. D3 compares G-SHAPE-F0's sources
    with the lock's tree, and the block itself says (L21-22) that "it is made again at the lock's commit". The ruling's
    "any other difference re-runs G-SHAPE-F0 first" therefore stays a conditional precondition at the lock. It is
    exactly the one item 1's other reading would trigger.
    
    Aside, not part of this claim: in the working tree, `data/m/gates/G-ANL-F0.superseded-2026-10-10.json` is untracked
    (??) and `G-ANL-F0.json` shows as modified, with nothing staged. So the "git mv" in L17 is not reflected in the
    index yet. It should be verified at commit time.
  CORRECTION:
    Beyond what they order first (G-CAL's close and the D2 round, both done, above) and D3's comparison, which is made
    again at the lock's commit ("any other difference re-runs G-SHAPE-F0 first"), the rulings set no precondition for
    LOCK v0.1 but the PI's word ("LOCK v0.1 is then Code's to draft and commit on the PI's word").
--- [H0-23] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    L32-33: item 2 is Code's reading, already in the code; any acceptance of it would be post hoc
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    I re-checked this independently, and the checker's verdict stands. Item 2 covers two things, and the block and the
    code at HEAD b7737ef give them different labels.
    - **The pause.** Block L72-74 says the end look "raises a pause if it fired" and "This is Code's application of
    m:330's 'A fired bar pauses'". That m:330 phrase is verbatim at m:330.
    - **The route.** Block L76-80 has the route through `--adjudication`, with "Code reads C3 as covering a STOP's
    pause".
    - **The code keeps them apart.**
      - m_analyze.py PIN-9 (register and inline): "Code's reading, flagged for the seat: a pause raised at a STOP (...;
    the pause is Code's application of m:330) is the campaign's last pause, so C3's route covers it too."
      - PIN-10: "a pause (m:330's 'A fired bar pauses', Code's application) and takes PIN-9's path (Code's reading of
    C3, flagged for the seat; see PIN-9)".
      - stages.py's `_campaign_events` docstring and the `_stop_end_look` PIN say the same.
    - **b7737ef's commit message** makes this its subject. It says the pause "is Code's application of m:330 ("A fired
    bar pauses"). C9(ii)'s words order only that the end look is evaluated, recorded and named". It also says "one label
    separates the STOP case (Code's reading of C3)".
    - **C9(ii) itself** (rulings L86) orders only evaluation, record and naming, and says nothing about a pause.

    keep exactly that distinction. The rest of the claim is right:
    - "already in the code": built at b8aa706, re-labelled by 185deb0, 97d70bd and b7737ef, and exercised by G-ANL-F0's
    re-run of record.
    - "any acceptance of it would be post hoc": rulings L3 says "where it accepts a reading Code acted on, the

    accurate. The wording below says the same thing, slightly tighter.
  CORRECTION:
    Item 2 is Code's and already in the code: the pause is Code's application of m:330, the route Code's reading of C3.

--- [H0-24] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    L34-35: an answer after the lock that goes against a default moves a hash the lock names, and every non-stub stage
    then refuses (D2) until a lock revision names the new one
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    I checked this myself at HEAD b7737ef, which has no diff under valence_core/ or in scripts/m_analyze.py. The
    checker's verdict stands.
    
    What the claim gets right:
    - All three stage drivers call `_licensed` → `_licence` → `licence_problems` at their start: run_gate_stage at
    L1472, run_gid_pair at L1736 and run_campaign at L2040. On a non-stub run, any problem raises SystemExit.
    - An analyzer move fails (d), the bytes checked against identity_extra's analyzer_sha256, or (f), the lock's
    committed text naming that hash.
    - A package move fails (e) or (f). The package hash is `resume.package_sha256` over valence_core/m/*.py, so
    gshape.py is included.
    - `prior_gate` lets a prior stage run under an earlier lock ("a prior under an EARLIER LOCK is allowed"), so a lock
    revision is enough for new stage starts.
    - Every against-default answer moves a hash the lock names: item 1 moves the package, item 2's strike moves the
    analyzer and maybe the package, and item 3 moves the analyzer.
    
    What it gets wrong is the blanket "(D2)". The seat's D2 (rulings L92) says only that "`analyzer_sha256` and
    `package_sha256` are required in the stages' `identity_extra` on non-stub runs, and the package hash is checked
    against the lock at stage start". Requiring the key cannot cause a refusal when the analyzer moves: a key carrying
    the new hash meets D2. The refusal comes from (d) or from (f)'s analyzer half. HEAD's `licence_problems` docstring
    says "(d) (finding R8) and (f)'s analyzer half (the PIN-14 precedent) are Code's", and the (f) refusal message says
    "the analyzer: Code's, after the analyzer's PIN-14". The block's own L105-106 says the same thing.
    
    So "(D2)" is right only for package moves: item 1, and item 2 if stages.py changes. For item 2's analyzer edit and
    for every item-3 edit, it credits the seat with Code's check. The same over-credit recurs at L113-114, and fully at
    L122-123, where only the analyzer moves. L64-65 (item 1, package only) is correct.
    
    One edge is not stated: a stage already running under the old hashes also refuses on resume, because
    `_record_licence` refuses a different licence. That does not make the claim wrong.
  CORRECTION:
    An answer after the lock that goes against a default moves a hash the lock names, and every non-stub stage then
    refuses at its start until a lock revision names the new one: by D2's check if the package moves, and by Code's half
    of it if the analyzer moves (finding R8, after the analyzer's PIN-14).
--- [H0-38] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    L72-73: that end look raises a pause if it fired: kind end_look, or kind look for a cadence look the hair trigger
    pre-empted
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The checker is right. Checked independently in valence_core/m/stages.py.
    
    - **The code at HEAD b7737ef, `_stop_end_look` (L2273-2325):** when n is a multiple of 40 and the look recorded at n
    exists, the end look is that look (`end_is_look`). Then `if gver or not res["fired"]: return end_look, None`. So
    when a G-VER STOP reuses a cadence look that fired, the function returns no pause. The only pauses it returns are:
      - kind "look", for a reused look under a hair trigger;
      - kind "end_look", for a newly evaluated end look that fired.
    - **The comment above the function** says the same: "after a G-VER STOP it was announced at the launch that reached
    n and any pause it raised was adjudicated before the next launch, so no new pause".
    - **Self-test (c) (L4015-4016)** covers this case: "a G-VER STOP at launch 41, after look 1 at 40 fired and was
    adjudicated: the end look IS look 1 ... no new pause".
    - **The logic has not changed since b8aa706.** `git diff b8aa706 b7737ef -- valence_core/m/stages.py` changes only
    comments and docstrings around this function, plus the refusal-message text. The line `if gver or not res["fired"]`
    is present at b8aa706, at its L2306.
    
    So "That end look raises a pause if it fired" overstates the rule: there is a reachable case where the end look
    fired and no pause is raised. The list of kinds after it does not fix this, because the sentence still says any
    fired end look pauses.
    
    The checker's own correction is close but has a small slip. Its parenthesis, "a cadence look a G-VER STOP reuses
    paused at its own launch", says that look always paused, which is only true if it fired.
  CORRECTION:
    That end look raises a pause if it fired: kind `end_look` for an end look evaluated at the STOP, or kind `look` for
    a cadence look the hair trigger pre-empted. The exception is a cadence look reused at a G-VER STOP: it raises no new
    pause, because it was announced at its own launch and any pause it raised was adjudicated before the next launch.
--- [H0-40] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    L74-75: when the STOP falls on a multiple of 40, reusing the cadence look recorded there as the end look is also
    Code's application
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The attribution is right. At HEAD b7737ef, the terminal branch's PIN in `_campaign_end` (valence_core/m/stages.py)
    says the end by STOP takes the same multiple-of-40 rule, and calls it "(_stop_end_look: Code's application to the
    end look the seat's C9(ii), 2026-10-10, orders at the STOP point)". The terminal multiple-of-40 rule is itself a
    Code PIN ("PIN (Code, declared at LOCK v0.1; m:330)"). The seat's C9(ii) (design-to-code rulings, line 86) says only
    "evaluated and recorded at the STOP point as the campaign's last look". It says nothing about reusing a cadence
    look.
    
    The condition in the claim is wrong. `_stop_end_look` reuses the look only when `n = len(ckpt["sessions"])` meets `n
    % LOOK_EVERY == 0 and last_look["at_launches"] == n`.
    - After a hair trigger, the STOP's session is recorded (with the cadence look due at its launch), so a STOP at
    launch 40 gives n = 40. That case matches "the STOP falls on a multiple of 40".
    - A G-VER failure's record is quarantined in the stop block and never counted.
      - A G-VER STOP at launch 40 gives n = 39. That evaluates a fresh end look, so nothing is reused, although the STOP
    falls on 40.
      - A G-VER STOP at launch 41 gives n = 40 and reuses look 1, although 41 is not a multiple of 40.
    
    Self-test (c) at HEAD covers exactly this case. Its label: "a G-VER STOP at launch 41, after look 1 at 40 fired and
    was adjudicated: the end look IS look 1 (end_is_look 1, at 40 -- the quarantined record not counted)". It asserts
    `len(sessions) == 40`, `end_is_look == 1` and `at_launches == 40`.
    
    The RESULTS entry draft (entry_d2.md, lines 50-55) states it correctly: "the end look is taken over the launches
    recorded at the STOP point ... a G-VER failure's record is quarantined and not counted ... When the count is a
    multiple of 40, the look recorded there is the end look". So the block's L74 wording is wrong for G-VER STOPs and
    doesn't match the entry. The checker's correction is right.
  CORRECTION:
    When the launches recorded at the STOP are a multiple of 40 (a G-VER STOP's quarantined record not counted), reusing
    the cadence look recorded there as the end look is also Code's application.

    (1) All three item-3 edits are in scripts/m_analyze.py. resume.package_sha256() hashes only valence_core/m/*.py
    (package_sha256_of(PACKAGE_DIR), resume.py:296-328), so an item-3 edit leaves the package hash unchanged. Checks (e)
    and (f)'s package half, which are D2's, cannot fire.
    
    (2) The refusal comes from licence_problems at stages.py:530-608, called by _licence at :611 and _licensed at :1139.
    Every driver passes through it: gate :1472, id :1736, campaign :2040. Two outcomes are possible:
    - identity_extra keeps the locked analyzer sha: (d) fires. The code text is "the analyzer's bytes hash to
    identity_extra's ``analyzer_sha256`` (finding R8)". At 8402174 this check already existed as an optional check
    ("when identity_extra pins analyzer_sha256", :530-561), so it predates D2.
    - identity_extra is updated to the new sha: (f)'s analyzer half fires. Its own message says "the analyzer: Code's,
    after the analyzer's PIN-14" (:606-607).
    
    (3) The HEAD docstring says "(d) (finding R8) and (f)'s analyzer half (the PIN-14 precedent) are Code's" (:543-544).
    The module docstring agrees: "the analyzer's half of the check is Code's, finding R8 and the analyzer's PIN-14
    precedent" (:32-33).
    
    (4) D2's words, in the seat's rulings at line 92: "`analyzer_sha256` and `package_sha256` are required in the
    stages' `identity_extra` on non-stub runs, and the package hash is checked against the lock at stage start", plus
    "the lock hashes the re-run's manifest and analyzer". So D2 does two things here. It makes the key required, which
    stops an operator from leaving out analyzer_sha256 to get past (d)/(f). It also puts the analyzer sha into the lock.
    It does not order the analyzer checked against its bytes or against the lock.
    
    (5) The block's own L105-106 says this: "`b7737ef` marks the analyzer half of the stage-start check as Code's:
    finding R8, after the analyzer's PIN-14. D2 orders both keys required, and the package hash checked against the
    lock."
    
    The mechanics in the claim are right: the sha the lock names stops matching, every non-stub stage refuses, and a
    lock revision clears it. But the bare "(D2)" gives the seat credit for a check it did not order. That is the class
    of error 185deb0, 97d70bd and b7737ef were made to remove, and it contradicts L105-106.
    
    The same over-attribution appears at L34-35, which covers item 3's after-lock answers, and at L113-114. Item 2's
    strike always edits the analyzer and only possibly stages.py, so the "(D2)" there is right only for the package
    half.
  CORRECTION:
    made after, the analyzer no longer hashes to the sha the lock names, so every non-stub stage refuses until a lock
    revision names the new one (the analyzer half of the stage-start check, which is Code's: finding R8, after the
    analyzer's PIN-14; D2 orders the key required);
    
    Parallel fixes in the same block:
    - L34-35: "... moves a hash the lock names, and every non-stub stage then refuses until a lock revision names the
    new one (D2 for the package; for the analyzer, Code's half of the check, D2 ordering the key required)."
    - L113-114: "... the same edits move hashes the lock names, and every non-stub stage refuses until a lock revision
    names the new ones (D2 for the package; Code's analyzer half of the check for the analyzer)."
--- [H1-24] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    L124-125: an edit made after LOCK-m-F0 v1.0 "also changes what PIN-14 makes the analyzer of record".
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The checker is right that the claim is wrong. Its own correction goes too far, though.
    
    1. The quote is exact. PIN-14 in the PINS dict of scripts/m_analyze.py at HEAD b7737ef (checked with ast; byte-
    identical to scratchpad step9/m_analyze_b7737ef.py) reads in full: "The analyzer of record for a unit is the blob
    hashed in that unit's LOCK v1.0; the lock names the analyzer's sha256, package_sha256 and the closure sha256, each
    asserted. When HEAD's three equal the lock's, VERDICTS runs at HEAD with no worktree; otherwise from a worktree of
    the lock commit with every data input and output passed as an absolute path into the main tree, and only on the PI's
    word for the worktree (not implemented at step 8). G-ANL-F3's versioning (m:291) is deferred by the seat to before
    step 19 (C7, 2026-10-10)."
    
    2. The consequence is wrong. An m_analyze.py edit made after LOCK-m-F0 v1.0 is by definition not "the blob hashed in
    that unit's LOCK v1.0". So it does not change what PIN-14 makes the analyzer of record. It splits HEAD off from that
    blob.
    
    3. The code shows what actually happens. In run_verdicts, about lines 1906-1924, the HEAD analyzer's sha256 is
    checked against the lock text with `if val not in ltext: raise AnlRefusal("REFUSED: the lock does not name this
    run's {what} (PIN-14, PIN-1)")`. VERDICTS at HEAD therefore refuses against that lock. PIN-14's route for this
    mismatch case is a worktree of the lock commit, on the PI's word, not implemented at step 8.
    
    4. The checker's replacement adds something no source provides. It says the edit "becomes the analyzer of record
    only through a revision of that unit's LOCK v1.0". Neither PIN-14 nor the proposal provides for that. m:286 and
    m:358 step 15 define LOCK-m-F0 v1.0 once, and a search of the proposal for "revis" / "lock revision" finds no LOCK
    v1.0 revision route. Stating that route as the answer settles a question the block should leave open; the block is
    meant to ask, not settle. The replacement below keeps only what PIN-14 and the code establish. Both quotes in it are
    exact substrings of PIN-14 at HEAD, checked programmatically.
  CORRECTION:
    - made after LOCK-m-F0 v1.0 (step 15), it also departs from the analyzer of record, which PIN-14 keeps as "the blob
    hashed in that unit's LOCK v1.0": VERDICTS refuses at HEAD against that lock, and would run that blob from a
    worktree of the lock commit, "only on the PI's word for the worktree (not implemented at step 8)".

    residue. The rows carry row_id/loc (L1417). This is consistent with "the residue the command lists".

--- [H1-37] IMPRECISE
  CLAIM:
    L157-158: "Until then, C7's bar is applied at the posting from the residue the command lists, as C7 orders."
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    The checker is right, so this is not refuted. C7's exact text (results/raw/2026-10-10_m-design-to-code_pre-
    LOCK-v0.1.md, line 81) reads: "PASS iff, on the posted text, the analyzer's enumeration under (a), (b) and (c) is
    empty ... and the dose note is present iff the flag; the enumeration of record is the VERDICTS one with the PI's
    rewrites applied, and any residue is listed by sentence and rewritten before posting. Fixed here, before the v1
    posting."
    
    C7 does order a bar for the posting's re-run, and it names "the analyzer's enumeration", which is what `posting`
    computes. In that narrow sense the sentence has a basis. But C7 says nothing about the command, and nothing about
    applying the bar from the command's list alone.
    
    Checked at HEAD b7737ef, scripts/m_analyze.py:
    - `enumeration()` (around line 1388) works from each row's declared `null_cells`, `thesis` and `dose_note`/`kinds`.
    It returns {row_id, loc{line, sentence, section}, clauses, ...}.
    - `run_posting()` (around line 2370) returns only the line "N enumerated rows (status: file)". It renders no
    PASS/FAIL.
    - Nothing compares the run with the VERDICTS enumeration.
    
    The block's own L135-140 lists these three gaps under "What does not" match. Item 3 asks "whether, and when" (a)
    should close them. Ending with "applied ... from the residue the command lists, as C7 orders" lends C7's authority
    to the claim that the command's list is enough to apply the bar. That quietly settles the very question item 3 puts
    to the seat.
    
    On m:349: Code enumerates the sentences under (a)/(b)/(c) "by the pre-committed analyzer where it exists ... and the
    PI rewrites each at posting". So "as C7 orders" is correct only when attached to listing and rewriting the residue,
    not to the way the bar is applied.
    
    The checker's proposed fix has two problems:
    - It invents a new step that no source declares: each row is "checked against" the VERDICTS enumeration.
    - "each row is mapped to its sentence" is redundant, because `loc` already carries the sentence index.
    
    My replacement below keeps only what the sources support. "The three gaps above" are the three bullets at L136-140.
  CORRECTION:
    Until then, at the posting C7's bar is applied by hand to the re-run's enumeration as the command lists it, and any
    residue is listed by sentence and rewritten before posting, as C7 orders. That the command's list, with the three
    gaps above, is enough to apply the bar is Code's reading.

--- (code-description)
  QUOTE:
    That end look raises a pause if it fired: kind `end_look`, or kind `look`
  PROBLEM:
    This overstates what the code does. In `_stop_end_look`, after a G-VER STOP whose recorded count is a multiple of
    40, the reused cadence look raises no new pause even if it fired (`if gver or not res["fired"]: return end_look,
    None`). That look was announced at its own launch, and any pause it raised was adjudicated before the next launch. A
    new pause is raised only for a fresh end look (kind `end_look`) or for a cadence look a hair trigger pre-empted
    (kind `look`).
  CORRECTION:
    "That end look raises a pause if it fired and none was raised for it already: kind `end_look` for a fresh end look,
    or kind `look` for a cadence look the hair trigger pre-empted. After a G-VER STOP on a multiple of 40, the reused
    look was announced at its own launch and any pause it raised was adjudicated then, so no new pause."
